python3\n\"\"\"\nConvert the Mesoscale Activity Map dataset (DANDI:000363, Chen et al. 2024) from NWB\ninto the decoder-ready pickle format.\n\nProcessing follows the reference pipeline of Wang, Kurgyis et al. 2025\n(`/app/code`, module `VideoAnalysisUtils/preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py` and\n`Sherlock/align_markers.py`):\n\n  * only units passing the spike-sorting quality-control classifier are used\n    (`units.classification == 'good'`, equivalent to the reference `goodunits` lists),\n  * everything is aligned to the **go cue**,\n  * spike trains are binned into firing rates (spikes / s) like `sliding_histogram(..., rate=True)`,\n  * neurons are labelled by hemisphere (CCF ML midline at 5700 um) + coarse CCF region,\n    exactly as in `helper_get_neuron_id_area`,\n  * side-view DeepLabCut markers (300 Hz) are aligned to the go cue as in `align_markers.py`.\n\nDeviations required by the decoder task are documented in /app/CONVERSION_NOTES.md.\n\nUsage:\n    python -u /app/convert_data.py <outpicklefile> [--full | --sample] [--show-processing]\n\"\"\"\n\nimport argparse\nimport glob\nimport os\nimport pickle\nimport sys\nimport time\nimport json\nimport warnings\nfrom multiprocessing import Pool\n\nimport numpy as np\n\nwarnings.filterwarnings('ignore')\n\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- config\nDATA_DIR = '/app/data'\n\nT_START = -2.5           # s relative to go cue (task specification)\nT_END = 1.5              # s relative to go cue\nBIN_SIZE = 0.05          # s (task specification: 50 ms bins)\nN_BINS = int(round((T_END - T_START) / BIN_SIZE))          # 80\nBIN_EDGES = T_START + BIN_SIZE * np.arange(N_BINS + 1)\nBIN_CENTERS = BIN_EDGES[:-1] + BIN_SIZE / 2\n\nVIDEO_DT = 0.0034        # s, 300 Hz side-view camera (reference `align_markers.py`)\nLIKELIHOOD_THRESH = 0.9  # DLC likelihood above which the tongue counts as visible\nMIN_VIDEO_COVERAGE = 0.9 # fraction of the window that must contain video frames\nMIN_SESSION_VIDEO_FRAC = 0.5  # a session must have video for at least this fraction of trials\nML_MIDLINE = 5700.0      # um, CCF ML midline (reference `helper_get_neuron_id_area`)\n\n# session selection criteria of the data paper (Chen et al., STAR Methods)\nMIN_PERFORMANCE = 0.65\nMIN_CORRECT_PER_DIRECTION = 50\n\nINPUT_NAMES = ['time_from_tone_onset', 'photostim_on']\nOUTPUT_NAMES = ['lick_direction_choice', 'outcome', 'early_lick', 'tongue_y_position']\nOUTPUT_VALUES = [['left', 'right', 'no lick'],\n                 ['ignore', 'miss', 'hit'],\n                 ['no', 'yes'],\n                 ['<40th pct', '40-60th pct', '>60th pct', 'not visible']]\n\nsys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))\nfrom region_map import coarse_region     # noqa: E402\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------------------------- helper functions\ndef bin_spike_times(spike_times_rel, n_neurons):\n    \"\"\"Bin go-cue-aligned spike times into firing rates.\n\n    Equivalent to the reference `sliding_histogram(..., rate=True)` with\n    bin_width == stride == BIN_SIZE.\n\n    Args:\n        spike_times_rel: list (len n_neurons) of 1-D arrays of spike times relative to\n            the go cue, already restricted to [T_START, T_END].\n        n_neurons: number of neurons.\n    Returns:\n        (n_neurons, N_BINS) float32 array of firing rates in spikes / s.\n    \"\"\"\n    out = np.zeros((n_neurons, N_BINS), dtype=np.float32)\n    for i, st in enumerate(spike_times_rel):\n        if st.size:\n            idx = np.floor((st - T_START) / BIN_SIZE).astype(np.int64)\n            np.clip(idx, 0, N_BINS - 1, out=idx)\n            out[i] = np.bincount(idx, minlength=N_BINS).astype(np.float32)\n    out /= BIN_SIZE\n    return out\n\n\ndef choice_from_trial(outcome, instruction):\n    \"\"\"Lick direction the animal actually chose: 0 left, 1 right, 2 no lick.\"\"\"\n    if outcome == 'ignore':\n        return 2\n    right = (instruction == 'right')\n    if outcome == 'miss':          # error trial -> licked the other spout\n        right = not right\n    return 1 if right else 0\n\n\nOUTCOME_CODE = {'ignore': 0, 'miss': 1, 'hit': 2}\n\n\ndef process_session(args):\n    \"\"\"Load, curate and convert one NWB session. Returns a dict or None.\"\"\"\n    path, show_processing, plot_dir = args\n    from pynwb import NWBHDF5IO\n\n    t_open = time.time()\n    timings = {}\n    with NWBHDF5IO(path, mode='r', load_namespaces=True) as io:\n        nwb = io.read()\n        sess_name = nwb.identifier\n        subject = nwb.subject.description or nwb.subject.subject_id\n        timings['open'] = time.time() - t_open\n\n        # ------------------------------------------------ trials table & task events\n        t0 = time.time()\n        trials = nwb.trials\n        n_trials = len(trials)\n        start_time = np.asarray(trials['start_time'].data[:])\n        stop_time = np.asarray(trials['stop_time'].data[:])\n        outcome = np.asarray(trials['outcome'].data[:])\n        instruction = np.asarray(trials['trial_instruction'].data[:])\n        early_lick = np.asarray(trials['early_lick'].data[:])\n        auto_water = np.asarray(trials['auto_water'].data[:])\n        free_water = np.asarray(trials['free_water'].data[:])\n\n        bev = nwb.acquisition['BehavioralEvents'].time_series\n        go_times_all = np.asarray(bev['go_start_times'].timestamps[:])\n        sample_times = np.asarray(bev['sample_start_times'].timestamps[:])\n        photostim_on = np.asarray(bev['photostim_start_times'].timestamps[:])\n        photostim_off = np.asarray(bev['photostim_stop_times'].timestamps[:])\n        timings['trials'] = time.time() - t0\n\n        # one go cue per trial (verified for every session in the dataset)\n        go = np.full(n_trials, np.nan)\n        gi = np.searchsorted(start_time, go_times_all, side='right') - 1\n        ok = (gi >= 0) & (gi < n_trials)\n        go[gi[ok]] = go_times_all[ok]\n        if np.any(np.isnan(go)):\n            return dict(sess=sess_name, skipped='missing go cue')\n\n        # --------------------------------------------- session-level behaviour checks\n        hit = outcome == 'hit'\n        miss = outcome == 'miss'\n        ctrl = ((early_lick == 'no early') & (auto_water == 0) & (free_water == 0)\n                & ~np.isin(np.arange(n_trials), np.searchsorted(start_time, photostim_on, side='right') - 1))\n        responded = (hit | miss) & ctrl\n        performance = hit[responded].sum() / max(responded.sum(), 1)\n        n_correct_left = int((hit & ctrl & (instruction == 'left')).sum())\n        n_correct_right = int((hit & ctrl & (instruction == 'right')).sum())\n\n        # ------------------------------------------------------------- unit curation\n        t0 = time.time()\n        units = nwb.units\n        classification = np.asarray(units['classification'].data[:])\n        good = np.where(classification == 'good')[0]\n        n_good = len(good)\n        info = dict(sess=sess_name, subject=subject, n_trials=n_trials, n_good=n_good,\n                    performance=float(performance), n_correct_left=n_correct_left,\n                    n_correct_right=n_correct_right)\n        if n_good == 0:\n            info['skipped'] = 'no good units'\n            return info\n        if not (performance > MIN_PERFORMANCE\n                and n_correct_left >= MIN_CORRECT_PER_DIRECTION\n                and n_correct_right >= MIN_CORRECT_PER_DIRECTION):\n            info['skipped'] = 'behavioural session criteria'\n            return info\n\n        anno = np.asarray(units['anno_name'].data[:])[good]\n        electrodes = nwb.electrodes\n        erows = np.asarray(units['electrodes'].target.data[:])[good]\n        ex = np.asarray(electrodes['x'].data[:])[erows]\n        egroup = np.asarray(electrodes['group_name'].data[:])[erows]\n        target_of_group = {}\n        for gname, grp in nwb.electrode_groups.items():\n            try:\n                target_of_group[gname] = json.loads(grp.location)['brain_regions']\n            except Exception:\n                target_of_group[gname] = ''\n        region_labels = []\n        for a, x, g in zip(anno, ex, egroup):\n            reg = coarse_region(str(a), target_of_group.get(g, ''))\n            side = 'left' if (np.isfinite(x) and x >= ML_MIDLINE) else 'right'\n            region_labels.append(f'{side} {reg}')\n        timings['units_meta'] = time.time() - t0\n\n        # --------------------------------------------------------------- video (tongue)\n        t0 = time.time()\n        bts = nwb.acquisition['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series\n        tongue = bts['Camera0_side_TongueTracking']\n        vtime = np.asarray(tongue.timestamps[:])\n        vdata = np.asarray(tongue.data[:])            # (n_frames, 3): x, y, likelihood\n        tongue_y = vdata[:, 1]\n        tongue_vis = vdata[:, 2] > LIKELIHOOD_THRESH\n        timings['video_read'] = time.time() - t0\n\n        # ---------------------------------------------------------------- spike times\n        t0 = time.time()\n        spikes = [np.asarray(units['spike_times'][int(i)]) for i in good]\n        timings['spikes_read'] = time.time() - t0\n\n        # ------------------------------------------------- observed (recorded) trials\n        # Spikes are only stored inside the NWB trial intervals and each unit carries\n        # `obs_intervals` listing the trials during which it was recorded. In 8 sessions\n        # the ephys covers only part of the behavioural session, so trials outside\n        # `obs_intervals` contain no neural data at all and must be dropped.\n        t0 = time.time()\n        observed = np.ones(n_trials, dtype=bool)\n        for i in good:\n            obs = np.asarray(units['obs_intervals'][int(i)])\n            oi = np.searchsorted(start_time, obs[:, 0], side='right') - 1\n            oi = oi[(oi >= 0) & (oi < n_trials)]\n            m = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=bool)\n            m[oi] = True\n            observed &= m\n        timings['obs_intervals'] = time.time() - t0\n        info['n_trials_observed'] = int(observed.sum())\n\n        # ------------------------------------------------------------- per-trial loop\n        t0 = time.time()\n        # tone onset = last sample-epoch onset before the go cue (epoch is replayed\n        # after an early lick, so the last one is the instructing tone)\n        tone_onset = np.full(n_trials, np.nan)\n        si = np.searchsorted(start_time, sample_times, side='right') - 1\n        for tr_i, s in zip(si, sample_times):\n            if 0 <= tr_i < n_trials and s < go[tr_i]:\n                if np.isnan(tone_onset[tr_i]) or s > tone_onset[tr_i]:\n                    tone_onset[tr_i] = s\n\n        # photostim windows per trial (relative to the go cue)\n        stim_on = np.full(n_trials, np.nan)\n        stim_off = np.full(n_trials, np.nan)\n        pi = np.searchsorted(start_time, photostim_on, side='right') - 1\n        for tr_i, on, off in zip(pi, photostim_on, photostim_off):\n            if 0 <= tr_i < n_trials:\n                stim_on[tr_i] = on - go[tr_i]\n                stim_off[tr_i] = off - go[tr_i]\n\n        win0 = go + T_START\n        win1 = go + T_END\n\n        # video coverage of the window, used for trial curation\n        vlo = np.searchsorted(vtime, win0)\n        vhi = np.searchsorted(vtime, win1)\n        coverage = (vhi - vlo) / ((T_END - T_START) / VIDEO_DT)\n\n        # ------------------------------------------------- session-level video check\n        # In 6 sessions the side-view video stops at (or before) the go cue, so the\n        # tongue output would be undefined for the whole response period.\n        if np.mean(coverage[observed] >= MIN_VIDEO_COVERAGE) < MIN_SESSION_VIDEO_FRAC:\n            info['skipped'] = 'video does not cover the analysis window'\n            return info\n\n        # ------------------------------------------------- trial selection mask\n        keep_mask = observed & (coverage >= MIN_VIDEO_COVERAGE) & np.isfinite(tone_onset)\n        n_dropped_unobserved = int((~observed).sum())\n        n_dropped_video = int((observed & (coverage < MIN_VIDEO_COVERAGE)).sum())\n        keep_trials = np.where(keep_mask)[0]\n        if len(keep_trials) == 0:\n            info['skipped'] = 'no usable trials'\n            return info\n        gk = go[keep_trials]\n        nk = len(keep_trials)\n\n        # ------------------------------------------------- neural: vectorised binning\n        # For every unit, all trial windows are extracted at once with searchsorted and\n        # binned with a single bincount (equivalent to the reference sliding_histogram\n        # with bin_width == stride == BIN_SIZE and rate=True).\n        neural_all = np.zeros((nk, n_good, N_BINS), dtype=np.float32)\n        w0 = gk + T_START\n        w1 = gk + T_END\n        for ui, st in enumerate(spikes):\n            if st.size == 0:\n                continue\n            lo = np.searchsorted(st, w0)\n            hi = np.searchsorted(st, w1)\n            cnt = hi - lo\n            tot = int(cnt.sum())\n            if tot == 0:\n                continue\n            trial_ids = np.repeat(np.arange(nk), cnt)\n            offsets = np.repeat(lo - np.concatenate(([0], np.cumsum(cnt)[:-1])), cnt)\n            pos = np.arange(tot) + offsets\n            rel = st[pos] - gk[trial_ids]\n            bidx = np.floor((rel - T_START) / BIN_SIZE).astype(np.int64)\n            np.clip(bidx, 0, N_BINS - 1, out=bidx)\n            flat = trial_ids * N_BINS + bidx\n            counts = np.bincount(flat, minlength=nk * N_BINS).reshape(nk, N_BINS)\n            neural_all[:, ui, :] = counts\n        neural_all /= BIN_SIZE      # spikes / s\n\n        # trials without a single spike from any good unit: recording interrupted\n        nonempty = neural_all.any(axis=(1, 2))\n        n_dropped_empty = int((~nonempty).sum())\n        keep_trials = keep_trials[nonempty]\n        neural_all = neural_all[nonempty]\n        gk = gk[nonempty]\n        nk = len(keep_trials)\n        if nk < 2:\n            info['skipped'] = 'fewer than 2 usable trials'\n            return info\n\n          sel = tongue_vis[lo:hi]\n            if not sel.any():\n                continue\n            vt = vtime[lo:hi][sel] - g\n            vy = tongue_y[lo:hi][sel]\n            bidx = np.floor((vt - T_START) / BIN_SIZE).astype(np.int64)\n            np.clip(bidx, 0, N_BINS - 1, out=bidx)\n            sums = np.bincount(bidx, weights=vy, minlength=N_BINS)\n            cnts = np.bincount(bidx, minlength=N_BINS)\n            nz = cnts > 0\n            tongue_bin_y[k, nz] = sums[nz] / cnts[nz]\n\n        # how much of the window lies after the end of the recorded trial interval\n        n_unobserved = int(np.sum(BIN_CENTERS[None, :] > (stop_time[keep_trials] - gk)[:, None]))\n\n        neural_trials = [neural_all[k] for k in range(nk)]\n        input_trials = [input_all[k] for k in range(nk)]\n        timings['bin_trials'] = time.time() - t0\n\n\n        # ------------------------------------------- per-session tongue discretization\n        visible = np.isfinite(tongue_bin_y)\n        if visible.sum() >= 10:\n            p40, p60 = np.percentile(tongue_bin_y[visible], [40, 60])\n        else:\n            p40 = p60 = np.nan\n        tongue_class = np.full(tongue_bin_y.shape, 3, dtype=np.int64)   # 3 = not visible\n        if np.isfinite(p40):\n            tongue_class[visible & (tongue_bin_y < p40)] = 0\n            tongue_class[visible & (tongue_bin_y >= p40) & (tongue_bin_y <= p60)] = 1\n            tongue_class[visible & (tongue_bin_y > p60)] = 2\n\n        # --------------------------------------------------------------- outputs\n        output_trials = []\n        for k, tr in enumerate(keep_trials):\n            out = np.zeros((4, N_BINS), dtype=np.int64)\n            out[0] = choice_from_trial(outcome[tr], instruction[tr])\n            out[1] = OUTCOME_CODE[outcome[tr]]\n            out[2] = 1 if early_lick[tr] == 'early' else 0\n            out[3] = tongue_class[k]\n            output_trials.append(out)\n\n        res = dict(\n            sess=sess_name, subject=subject, path=path,\n            neural=neural_trials, input=input_trials, output=output_trials,\n            region_labels=region_labels,\n            n_trials_total=n_trials, n_trials_kept=len(keep_trials), n_good=n_good,\n            performance=float(performance), n_correct_left=n_correct_left,\n            n_correct_right=n_correct_right,\n            trial_idx=keep_trials,\n            frac_unobserved_bins=float(n_unobserved / (len(keep_trials) * N_BINS)),\n            tongue_p40=float(p40), tongue_p60=float(p60),\n            n_photostim=int(np.isfinite(stim_on[keep_trials]).sum()),\n            n_trials_observed=int(observed.sum()),\n            n_dropped_video=int(n_dropped_video), n_dropped_unobserved=int(n_dropped_unobserved),\n            n_dropped_empty=int(n_dropped_empty),\n            n_auto_water=int(auto_water[keep_trials].sum()),\n            n_free_water=int(free_water[keep_trials].sum()),\n            timings=timings,\n        )\n\n        if show_processing:\n            plot_session(nwb, res, go, tone_onset, stim_on, stim_off, vtime=vtime,\n                         tongue_y=tongue_y, tongue_vis=tongue_vis, spikes=spikes,\n                         tongue_bin_y=tongue_bin_y, plot_dir=plot_dir)\n        return res\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------------------------------- plotting\ndef plot_session(nwb, res, go, tone_onset, stim_on, stim_off, vtime, tongue_y,\n                 tongue_vis, spikes, tongue_bin_y, plot_dir):\n    \"\"\"Visualise every processing step for one session.\"\"\"\n    import matplotlib\n    matplotlib.use('Agg')\n    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n\n    sess = res['sess']\n    keep = res['trial_idx']\n    ntr_plot = min(3, len(keep))\n    # prefer trials with photostim / visible tongue for the illustration\n    order = np.argsort(-np.array([res['output'][k][3].min() < 3 for k in range(len(keep))], dtype=float))\n    sel = order[:ntr_plot]\n\n    fig, axes = plt.subplots(5, ntr_plot, figsize=(6 * ntr_plot, 16), squeeze=False)\n    for j, k in enumerate(sel):\n        tr = keep[k]\n        g = go[tr]\n        # 1. spike raster (raw times minus go cue) for up to 40 neurons\n        ax = axes[0, j]\n        for color='k', markersize=2)\n        ax.axvline(0, color='r', label='go cue')\n        ax.axvline(tone_onset[tr] - g, color='g', label='tone onset')\n        if np.isfinite(stim_on[tr]):\n            ax.axvspan(stim_on[tr], stim_off[tr], color='b', alpha=0.2, label='photostim')\n        ax.set_title(f'{sess}\\ntrial {tr}: raw spikes aligned to go cue')\n        ax.set_xlim(T_START, T_END);                  extent=[T_START, T_END, 0, min(40, res['n_good'])], cmap='viridis')\n        ax.axvline(0, color='r')\n        ax.set_title('binned firing rate (spikes/s), 50 ms bins')\n        ax.set_ylabel('neuron')\n        plt.colorbar(im, ax=ax)\n\n        # 3. inputs\n        ax = axes[2, j]\n        ax.plot(BIN_CENTERS, res['input'][k][0], label='time from tone onset (s)')\n        ax.plot(BIN_CENTERS, res['input'][k][1], label='photostim on')\n        ax.axvline(0, color='r'); ax.axvline(tone_onset[tr] - g, color='g')\n        ax.set_title('inputs'); ax.legend(fontsize=7)\n\n        # 4. raw tongue trace vs binned class\n        ax = axes[3, j]\n        lo = np.searchsorted(vtime, g + T_START); hi = np.searchsorted(vtime, g + T_END)\n        t = vtime[lo:hi] - g\n        yy dpi=110)\n    plt.close(fig)\n\n    # session-level summary figure: tongue percentile discretization + coverage\n    fig, axes = plt.subplots(1, 3, figsize=(18, 4.5))\n    vis = axes[0].axvline(res['tongue_p60'], color='C3', ls='--', label='60th pct')\n    axes[0].set_title(f'{sess}: binned tongue y (visible bins)'); axes[0].legend()\n    cls = np.stack([o[3] for o in {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01NUrQB5pKgGK96yefbzd1yb","type":"tool_result","content":"Behavior and video tracking\n\nMice performed an auditory delayed response task 21 (Figure 1A). The instruction stimuli during the sample epoch were pure tones played at one of two frequencies (3 kHz or 12 kHz). Each tone was played three times for 150 ms with 100 ms inter-tone intervals. The sample epoch was followed by a 1.2 s delay epoch. An auditory ‘Go’ cue (carrier frequency of 6 kHz with 360 Hz modulating fre-\nquency, 0.1 s duration) indicated the end of the delay epoch. Licking early during the sample/delay epoch triggered a replay of the epoch. During the response epoch (answer period: 1.5 s) mice reported the instruction by licking one of the two lick ports. Licking (consumption period: 1.5 s) the correct lick port triggered a small water reward (0.1 ~ 0.2 ul). Licking the incorrect lick port triggered a timeout (1-3 s). After mice stopped licking for 1.5 s, the trial ended, followed by a 250 ms inter-trial-interval. Early lick trials and no\nresponse trials were excluded for analysis. Overall performance was computed as the fraction of correct control trials (i.e. no photostimulation), excluding any early lick trials. We selected experimental sessions for analysis based on following criteria: overall behavioral performance (> 65%), and at least 50 correct lick left and lick right trials each. Mice performed on average 476 (Mean; range, 130-785) trials per session with 84% correct rate (range, 65-99%; STAR Methods). During the delay epoch, mice maintain a memory of their choice and plan a movement.\n\nTwo CMOS cameras (CM3-U3-13Y3M, FLIR) were used to track orofacial movements of the mouse under IR light illumination (940 nm LED). The cameras were equipped with 4-12 mm focal length lenses (12VM412ASIR, Tamron) and a pixel resolution of 71 mm. High-speed videos from a side view and a bottom view (Figure 1C) were acquired at 300 Hz using software FlyCapture (TELEDYNE FLIR). We trained DeepLabCut 39 to track the movement of tongue, jaw and nose (Figure 1I).\n\nMulti-regional electrophysiological recordings\n\nWe designed a ﬂexible manipulator system for multiple Neuropixels probe insertions (https://www.janelia.org/open-science/manipulator-system-for-multiple-neuropixels-probe-recordings). Three to four small craniotomies (diameter, 1 \u0018 1.5 mm) were made over target brain areas one day before the ﬁrst recording session. Details of recordings made with multiple Neuropixels probes in head-restrained behaving animals have been published (dx.doi.org/10.17504/protocols.io.8tphwmn). Target regions, insertions angles, probe types, etc. are summarized in Table S2. All coordinates are given with respect to Bregma (AP, anterior-posterior; ML, medial-lateral; DV, dorsal-ventral).\n\nExtracellular spikes were recorded using Neuropixels 1.0 probes, 2.0 single-shank (SS) and 2.0 multi-shank (MS) probes 3,4 (Table S2). Three to seven recordings were made from each craniotomy. Recording depth was inferred from manipulator readings (Sensapax uMp manipulators). After completion of probe insertion, brain tissue was allowed to settle for several minutes before recording. During recordings the craniotomies were immersed in saline.\n\nProbes were conﬁgured and data were visualized and streamed to disk using the open-source software package SpikeGLX (https://billkarsh.github.io/SpikeGLX/). Data from up to ﬁve simultaneously recorded probes were acquired at 30 kHz. Sync waves (0.5 s duty cycle TTL pulses) were recorded across probes and on auxiliary channels for synchronization.\n\nPhotoinhibition\n\nLight from two 473 nm lasers (Coherent OBIS LS/LX) was used to illuminate the two hemispheres of ALM. The laser power was controlled by analog outputs controlled by a Bpod (Sanworks). Photoinactivation of ALM (centered on AP 2.5 mm; ML 1.5 mm) was performed through clear-skull cap implant11 or via craniotomy by directing the laser over the skull (beam diameter, 1.8 mm at 4 sigma). Photoinhibition of the left-, right-ALM hemisphere, or both hemispheres was deployed on a subset of ~25% randomly interleaved trials 11,21 (N = 17 VGAT-ChR2-EYFP mice). To prevent the mice from distinguishing photostimulation trials from control rials using visual cues, a ‘masking ﬂash’ (1 ms pulses at 10 Hz) was delivered using 470 nm LEDs in front of the mice throughout the entire trial. For silencing ALM, we stimulated cortical GABAergic neurons in VGAT-ChR2-EYFP mice. We used 40 Hz photostimulation with a sinusoidal temporal proﬁle (5 mW average power per hemisphere) and a 100 ms linear ramping down during the laser offset to reduce rebound neuronal activity. This photo stimulus is expected to silence glutamatergic neurons in all of ALM. 37 We silenced ALM activity during the late delay epoch (last 0.5 s), including the 100 ms ramp-down. Thus, photoinhibition always ended before the ‘Go’ cue.\n\nTo analyze the effects of photoinhibition, units with at least 10 photostimulation trials were selected for analysis (Figures 5 and S5). Bilateral ALM photostimulation reduced behavior performance from 83.2% to 71.7% (N = 17 VGAT-ChR2-EYFP mice, n = 93 sessions). To exclude the effects of rebound population activity in the thalamus 16 and other subcortical regions, we used a 20-120 ms\ntime window after photo stimulation onset to measure the effect of ALM inactivation in downstream regions (Figures 5 and S5). For ALM itself, we used 500 ms (i.e. the entire inactivation period) to calculate spike rate reduction.\n\nSpike sorting and quality control\n\nWe developed a data pipeline for preprocessing, spike sorting and quality control (https://github.com/jenniferColonell/ecephys_spike_sorting). The extracellular recording traces were ﬁrst band-pass ﬁltered (Butterworth acausal ﬁlter, 250 Hz - 9 kHz). We then sorted the dataset using both Kilosort2 32 (MATLAB version, GitHub - jenniferColonell/KS20_for_preprocessed_data: Release version of KS2 for preprocessed data) and Kilosort2.5 4 (Python version, GitHub - jenniferColonell/pykilosort: [WIP] Python port of Kilosort 2). Both sorting methods gave similar results. In the analyses presented in the paper, we used the output from Kilosort2. Each output cluster was associated with a list of 15 quality metrics calculated to assess isolation and sorting quality 7 (i.e. quality control) (https://github.com/AllenInstitute/ecephys_spike_sorting/tree/master/ecephys_spike_sorting/modules/quality_metrics).\n\nThe details of the spike sorting and quality control are described in an accompanying white paper (https://doi.org/10.25378/janelia.24066108.v1). In brief, we used 15 cluster quality metrics to train classiﬁers - one for each of the major ﬁve brain areas (cortex, striatum, thalamus, midbrain and medulla). The classiﬁers were trained based on labels derived from manual curation. We curated Kilosort2 output (28 penetrations from ﬁve brain areas) using the open-source graphical user interface Phy (GitHub - cortex-lab/phy: phy: interactive visualization and manual spike sorting of large-scale ephys data). Each cluster was labeled as a ‘good’ or ‘unlabeled’ unit. Annotators were blind to location, recording condition and trial type information. Based on the annotated labels of all clusters and associated quality metrics, we trained ﬁve region-speciﬁc classiﬁers using logistic regression. Applying the trained classiﬁers to the rest of the dataset provided lists of units that were labeled as ‘good’ and were used for the analysis in this paper. We applied the cortex classiﬁer to ALM and other cortex, hippocampus, olfactory and cortical subplate units; the striatum classiﬁer to striatum and pallidum units; the thalamus classiﬁer to thalamus and hypothalamus units; the midbrain classiﬁer to midbrain and pons units; the medulla classiﬁer to medulla and cerebellum units. We calculated the rate at which the classiﬁer incorrectly labeled ’unlabeled’ units as ’good’, using 10-fold cross-validation on held-out manually labeled units (false alarm rate): 7.8%; striatum: 6.4%; thalamus: 7.3%; midbrain: 5.5%; medulla 4.3%. This method yielded 8717 good units from ALM, 7664 from striatum, 12808 from thalamus, 7495 from midbrain and 2928 from medulla, etc. Overall, the dataset consisted of 69,943 good units recorded across 173 behavioral sessions, from which 655 probe insertions were made. This corresponds to 25.9 % of clusters reported by Kilosort2.","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"97078935-1b5c-4232-a1bf-e7856a20b94f","uuid":"11d83a2b-5649-4daa-8207-0f7808f8d3f3","timestamp":"2026-09-21T05:33:42.786Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"Behavior and video tracking\n\nMice performed an auditory delayed response task 21 (Figure 1A). It operates on `.mat`\nexports of the same DataJoint database that DANDI:000363 NWB files were generated from, so field names\nmap closely onto NWB fields.\n\n### Key Functions Identified\n| Function | File | Stage | Purpose |\n|----------|------|-------|---------|\n| `process_all_sess_parallel` / `process_one_sess` | VideoAnalysisUtils/preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py | LOADING | Loop over sessions; per session concatenate all probe files; read behaviour/task variables and spike times |\n| `sliding_histogram(spikeTimes, begin, end, bin_width, stride, rate=True)` | preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py | PROCESSING | Bin spike times (already aligned so go cue = 0) into sliding-window firing rates; returns bin centers and `fr` (n_bins, n_trials, n_neurons) in spikes/s |\n| `process_one_area` | preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py | PROCESSING | Truncate spike times to [begin_time, end_time], bin them, save per-area pickle with behaviour fields |\n| `helper_get_neuron_id_area` | preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py | CURATION | Keep only units that (a) passed QC ('goodunits' classifier list) and (b) fall in given hemisphere (CCF ML coordinate vs 5700 µm midline) and region annotation |\n| `get_regular_trial_mask` | population_decoding_utils.py, functions_for_r2.py | CURATION | Regular trials: `early_lick==0 & auto_water==0 & free_water==0 & correctness!=-1 & stimulation[:,0]==0` |\n| `create_4fold_trial_type_mask` | functions_for_r2.py | CURATION | Stratify trials into hit-right/miss-right/hit-left/miss-left for CV |\n| `align_markers_between_lims` | Sherlock/align_markers.py | PROCESSING | Align DeepLabCut side-camera markers (`nose_x/y, tongue_x/y, jaw_x/y, whisker_x/y`) to the go cue: frame time = frame_index*dt − go_cue_time, dt = 0.0034 s (≈300 Hz); resample onto grid `np.arange(-3, 1.5, dt)` taking the last frame in each bin (0 if none) |\n| `get_bad_trial_inds` | Sherlock/align_markers.py | CURATION | Flag trials where #video frames disagrees with trial end time by more than one frame |\n| `get_period` | preprocessing_utils.py | PROCESSING | Epoch definitions relative to go cue: sample −1.9…−1.2 s, delay −1.2…0 s, post-go 0…1 s, all −3.0…3.5 s |\n| `get_single_area_inds` / `get_inds_for_list_of_regions` | functions.py | PROCESSING | Map CCF annotations onto coarse brain-region groups (ALM handled specially via ALM voxel mask / qc list) |\n| `nested_cross_validation` | population_decoding_utils.py | ANALYSIS | Logistic-regression population decoding (PCA 16 comps) per time bin, AUC scored |\n\n### Notes / important parameters from reference code\n- `Sherlock/preprocess_all_ephys.py`: `bw = 0.04 s`, `stride = 0.0034 s`, `begin_time = -3.0`, `end_time = 3.0`, `qc_mode = 'classifier'` (for video-matched analyses; the module `__main__` block uses bw=0.1, stride=0.05, −3.0…3.5).\n- Spike times in the source (Susu's export / NWB) are **relative to the go cue** for each trial.\n- Firing rates are computed as counts / bin_width (spikes per second).\n- Units are curated by the **'classifier' QC** described in Chen, Liu et al. 2023 white paper (`goodunits` folder), i.e. only units passing quality control are used.\n- Regions in the reference pipeline: ALM, Medulla, Midbrain, Striatum, Thalamus, Pons, Cerebellum, Hypothalamus, Hippocampus, Orbital, OtherCortex, Olfactory, CorticalSubplate, Pallidum; each split into left/right hemisphere using CCF ML midline 5700 µm.\n- Trial curation for their encoding/decoding analyses keeps only \"regular\" trials (no early lick, no auto/free water, no no-response, **no photostimulation**). For the present decoder task photostimulation must be an input and early lick / ignore outcomes must be outputs, so these trial exclusions **cannot** be applied here (documented deviation).\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n`/app/data` is a DANDI download of **dandiset 000363** (\"Mesoscale Activity Map Dataset\", Chen, Nguyen, Li, Svoboda 2023),\norganised as `sub-<subject_id>/sub-<subject_id>_ses-<YYYYMMDD>T<HHMMSS>_behavior+ecephys+ogen.nwb`.\n\n- **174 NWB files** = 174 sessions, **28 subjects** (`sub-440956` … `sub-484677`), 50 GB total.\n- Every file read with `pynwb.NWBHDF5IO(..., load_namespaces=True)`.\n\nContents of each NWB file (verified on several files):\n\n| NWB location | Contents |\n|---|---|\n| `nwb.identifier` | session name, e.g. `SC015_20190207_120657_s1` (mouse, date, time, session #) — same naming used by the reference `.mat` exports |\n| `nwb.subject` | `subject_id` (e.g. 440956), `description` = mouse nickname (e.g. `SC015`), sex, DOB, species |\n| `nwb.trials` (TimeIntervals) | columns: `start_time`, `stop_time`, `trial`, `photostim_onset`, `photostim_power`, `photostim_duration` (strings, `'N/A'` when no photostim; onset is **relative to trial start**), `trial_uid`, `task` (always `audio delay`), `task_protocol`, `trial_instruction` (`left`/`right`), `early_lick` (`early`/`no early`), `outcome` (`hit`/`miss`/`ignore`), `auto_water` (0/1), `free_water` (0/1) |\n| `nwb.acquisition['BehavioralEvents']` | TimeSeries with **timestamps in session clock**: `presample_start/stop_times`, `sample_start/stop_times`, `delay_start/stop_times`, `go_start/stop_times`, `trialend_start/stop_times`, `left_lick_times`, `right_lick_times`, `photostim_start_times` (data = laser power in mW), `photostim_stop_times` |\n| `nwb.acquisition['BehavioralTimeSeries']` | DeepLabCut side-view tracking at 300 Hz (dt = 0.0034 s): `Camera0_side_TongueTracking`, `Camera0_side_JawTracking`, `Camera0_side_NoseTracking`, each (n_frames, 3) = (x, y, likelihood), with **session-clock timestamps**; a few sessions also have `Camera0_side_WhiskerTracking_whisker` (20), `LickPortTracking` (4) or `Camera3_*` (3) |\n| `nwb.units` | 39 columns incl. `spike_times` (session clock), `obs_intervals`, `unit_quality` (`good`/`multi`, the Kilosort/Phy label), **`classification`** (`good` / `unlabelled` — the QC-classifier label from the Chen–Liu white paper), **`anno_name`** (CCF structure name, only non-empty for `classification=='good'` units), `is_good_trials` (bool per trial, per unit), 15 QC metrics (`presence_ratio`, `amplitude_cutoff`, `isi_violation`, `unit_snr`, `drift_metric`, `avg_firing_rate`, …), `electrodes` (DynamicTableRegion → 1 electrode per unit), `electrode_group` |\n| `nwb.electrodes` | `x`, `y`, `z` = CCF coordinates in µm (x = ML, midline at 5700 µm — **left hemisphere is x ≥ 5700**, verified against probe target labels), `group_name`, `shank`, … |\n| `nwb.electrode_groups[*].location` | JSON with targeted `brain_regions` (e.g. `left ALM`, `right Striatum`), insertion coordinates and angles |\n\n### Dataset Size (from data files; computed with `cache/scan_all.py`, `cache/scan_units.py`)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| NWB files / sessions | 174 |\n| Subjects | 28 |\n| Sessions / subject | mean 6.2 (range 1–13) |\n| Trials (total) | 94,990 |\n| Trials / session | mean 545.9 (range 264–800) |\n| Units (all, incl. `unlabelled`) | 272,227 |\n| **Good units (`classification=='good'`)** | **69,453** (25.5 % of all clusters) |\n| Good units / session | mean 399.2 (range 0–923); **1 session has 0 good units** (`SC017_20190216_162508_s4`) |\n| Probe insertions (electrode groups) | 659 (targets: ALM 211, Midbrain 136, Striatum 114, Thalamus 91, Medulla 58, ECT 32, BLA 17) |\n| Unique CCF `anno_name` values (good units) | 293 |\n| Trials with photostim | 18,588 (19.6 %) in 168 sessions |\n| Outcomes | hit 65,254 (68.7 %), miss 15,641 (16.5 %), ignore 14,095 (14.8 %) |\n| Early lick | early 10,805 (11.4 %), no early 84,185 |\n| Instruction | right 48,913, left 46,077 |\n| Sessions with side-view tongue tracking | 174 (all) |\n\n### Additional structural facts established by exploration\n- Exactly **one go cue per trial** (`go_start_times` length == n_trials in all 174 sessions).\n- `sample_start_times` can occur **several times per trial** (tone epoch is replayed after an early lick): mean 3.06 sample events on early-lick trials vs exactly 1 on other trials. Time from **last** sample onset to go cue ≈ 1.85 s (0.65 s sample + 1.2 s delay); time from **first** sample onset to go cue can be much longer on replay trials.\n- `photostim_start_times`/`photostim_stop_times` reproduce `trials.start_time + photostim_onset` exactly (`np.allclose`, atol 1e-3). Photostim is delivered in the **late delay** (e.g. −1.2 → −0.7 s relative to go cue) and always ends before the go cue.\n- Video timestamps are in the **session clock**, one continuous 300 Hz block per trial (n_gaps = n_trials − 1), starting at each trial start — consistent with the reference code's `frame_index*dt − go_time` alignment.\n- 6 sessions (`SC011_2019022[456]`, `SC022_2019022[78]`, `SC066_20210413`) have video that stops at/near the go cue (post-go coverage 1–6 %), and `SC066_20210413_112028_s6` has essentially no usable video (1 %); `SC065_20210505_170309_s6` covers 62 %.\n- Tongue `likelihood` is strongly bimodal (≈ 1 when visible, ≈ 1e-4 when not). Frames with likelihood > 0.9: 11.7 % on average (range 2.9–73 %).\n- `units.is_good_trials` was all-`True` for every good unit checked (9 sessions × up to 200 units) → no additional per-unit trial masking needed (re-verified over the full dataset in Step 10).\n- Spike times are in the **session clock** (0 … session end) — unlike the reference `.mat` export where they were already aligned to the go cue; therefore alignment to the go cue must be done here.\n\n---\n\n## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nSources read: `/app/methods.txt`, `datapaper.pdf` (Chen et al. 2024, *Brain-wide neural activity underlying\nmemory-guided movement*), `methodpaper.pdf` (Wang, Kurgyis et al. 2025, *Brain-wide analysis reveals movement\nencoding structured across and within brain areas*). PDFs converted to text in `cache/datapaper.txt`,\n`cache/methodpaper.txt`.\n\n### Expected Statistics (from papers/methods)\n| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n|-----------|-------|--------------|\n| Good units (total) | 69,943 | \"the dataset consisted of 69,943 good units recorded across 173 behavioral sessions\" (methods.txt) |\n| Good units by area | ALM 8,717; striatum 7,664; thalamus 12,808; midbrain 7,495; medulla 2,928 | methods.txt |\n| Fraction of clusters that are 'good' | 25.9 % | \"This corresponds to 25.9 % of clusters reported by Kilosort2\" |\n| Sessions | 173 behavioral sessions | methods.txt; data paper Fig 1J \"660 penetrations, 173 behavioral sessions, and 28 mice\" |\n| Subjects | 28 mice (25 VGAT-ChR2-EYFP, 1 C57BL/6J, 1 Sst-IRES-Cre×Ai32, 1 Emx1-Cre×GtACR1) | method paper Methods |\n| Probe insertions | 660 (data paper) / 655 (methods.txt) | Fig 1J legend |\n| Trials / session | mean 476, range 130–785 | \"Mice performed on average 476 (Mean; range, 130-785) trials per session\" |\n| Behavioural performance | 84 % correct, range 65–99 % | same sentence |\n| Session selection (papers) | performance > 65 % **and** ≥ 50 correct lick-left and ≥ 50 correct lick-right trials | \"We selected experimental sessions for analysis based on following criteria: overall behavioral performance (> 65%), and at least 50 correct lick left and lick right trials each.\" |\n| Photostim trials | ~25 % randomly interleaved, N = 17 VGAT-ChR2 mice, 93 sessions | methods.txt Photoinhibition |\n| Photostim effect | performance 83.2 % → 71.7 % (bilateral ALM) | methods.txt |\n| Neural bin width / stride (method paper) | **40 ms bin width, 3.4 ms stride** | \"we binned spikes into firing rates with a bin width of 40 ms and a stride of 3.4 ms\" |\n| Video frame rate | 300 Hz (dt = 1/300 ≈ 0.0034 s), side view, DeepLabCut markers (tongue, jaw, nose, paws) | method paper Methods |\n| Trial epochs | sample 0.65 s (3 × 150 ms tones + 100 ms gaps), delay 1.2 s, go cue 0.1 s, answer/response 1.5 s, consumption 1.5 s | methods.txt Behavior |\n| Epoch windows used in reference code | sample −1.9…−1.2 s, delay −1.2…0 s, response 0…1.0/1.5 s relative to go cue | `preprocessing_utils.get_period`, `functions_for_r2.process_single_session_r2_dict` |\n\n### Processing Details\n- **Temporal alignment**: everything is aligned to the **go cue** (t = 0). The reference `.mat` export already stored spike times relative to the go cue; in the NWB files spike times are in session time, so we subtract `go_start_times`.\n- **Binning**: sliding histogram, firing rate = spike count / bin width (spikes/s). Reference used bw = 40 ms with 3.4 ms stride (method paper) or bw = 100 ms/stride 50 ms in the module `__main__`. **Our task specifies 50 ms bins**, so we use non-overlapping 50-ms bins (stride = width = 50 ms), which matches the spirit (rate in spikes/s) while satisfying the decoder spec.\n- **Trial window**: reference used −3.0 → 3.0 / 3.5 s around the go cue; **our task specifies −2.5 → +1.5 s**.\n- **Video**: markers aligned to the go cue with frame time = frame_index × 0.0034 − go_time; reference resamples onto a −3…1.5 s grid taking the last frame in each bin. Outliers (5-sigma on velocity) imputed; when the tongue is occluded (inside the mouth) they set the tongue position to its mean value. For our decoder the occluded state is its own output class (`not visible`), so we detect occlusion from the DLC `likelihood` rather than imputing.\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules** (papers + white paper `ChenLiuEtAl2023_SpikeSortingQC.pdf`):\n- Spike sorting with Kilosort2; 15 quality metrics; five region-specific logistic-regression classifiers (cortex, striatum, thalamus, midbrain, medulla) trained on manual Phy labels; units labelled **'good'** by the classifier are the ones used in all analyses. In NWB this is exactly `units.classification == 'good'` (69,453 units in the 174 files ≈ the 69,943 reported).\n- Method paper additionally drops neurons with mean firing rate < 2 Hz *for the encoding (R²) analyses* and brain areas with < 10 neurons per session. These are analysis-specific thresholds, not dataset curation; we do **not** apply them (a population decoder benefits from all good units, and dropping low-rate units would discard information).\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n- Papers exclude, *for their analyses*: photoinhibition trials, free-water/auto-water trials, early-lick trials and no-response ('ignore') trials (`get_regular_trial_mask`).\n- **Our decoder task requires photostimulation as an input and early lick / ignore outcome as outputs**, so these trials must be kept. This is the single deliberate, documented deviation from the reference curation. Auto-water / free-water trials are also kept because dropping them would remove no needed variable; they are flagged in the metadata (they are rare: see Step 9).\n- Session-level: the papers keep sessions with performance > 65 % and ≥ 50 correct left and ≥ 50 correct right trials (145/174 sessions satisfy this in the raw data).\n\n### Decoders Trained (reference results to compare against)\n| Decoded variable | Method | Accuracy |\n|---|---|---|\n| Choice (lick direction) from **video**, pre-sample epoch | logistic regression on video embeddings | AUC 0.51 ± 0.06 (n = 106 sessions) |\n| Choice from **video**, sample+delay | same | AUC 0.66 ± 0.12 |\n| Choice from **video**, 2nd half of response epoch | same | AUC 0.99 ± 0.01 |\n| Choice from **neural population** (200 neurons, 200 ms causal window) | logistic regression, hierarchical bootstrap | ALM rises during sample/delay; ≈ 0.9 late delay (data paper Fig 6D) |\n| Choice from ALM population, late delay | data paper Fig S6 | ~0.8–0.95 depending on n neurons |\n\nExpectation for our decoder: with all good units of a session and the full −2.5…+1.5 s window, **choice and\noutcome should be decodable well above chance (≥ 0.8 balanced accuracy)**, tongue y-position (which is a direct\nreadout of licking) should be high after the go cue, and early lick should be decodable above chance but is\nharder (11 % base rate, event occurs before the window centre).\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nI cross-checked the three sources (reference code, NWB data, papers). onset is **relative to trial start**), `trial_uid`, `task` (always `audio delay`), `task_protocol`, `trial_instruction` (`left`/`right`), `early_lick` (`early`/`no early`), `outcome` (`hit`/`miss`/`ignore`), `auto_water` (0/1), `free_water` (0/1) |\n| `nwb.acquisition['BehavioralEvents']` | TimeSeries with **timestamps in session clock**: `presample_start/stop_times`, `sample_start/stop_times`, `delay_start/stop_times`, `go_start/stop_times`, `trialend_start/stop_times`, `left_lick_times`, `right_lick_times`, `photostim_start_times` (data = laser power in mW), `photostim_stop_times` |\n| `nwb.acquisition['BehavioralTimeSeries']` | DeepLabCut side-view tracking at 300 Hz (dt = 0.0034 s): `Camera0_side_TongueTracking`, `Camera0_side_JawTracking`, `Camera0_side_NoseTracking`, each (n_frames, 3) = (x, y, likelihood), with **session-clock timestamps**; a few sessions also have `Camera0_side_WhiskerTracking_whisker` (20), `LickPortTracking` (4) or `Camera3_*` (3) |\n| `nwb.units` | 39 columns incl. `spike_times` (session clock), `obs_intervals`, `unit_quality` (`good`/`multi`, the Kilosort/Phy label), **`classification`** (`good` / `unlabelled` — the QC-classifier label from the Chen–Liu white paper), **`anno_name`** (CCF structure name, only non-empty for `classification=='good'` units), `is_good_trials` (bool per trial, per unit), 15 QC metrics (`presence_ratio`, `amplitude_cutoff`, `isi_violation`, `unit_snr`, `drift_metric`, `avg_firing_rate`, …), `electrodes` (DynamicTableRegion → 1 electrode per unit), `electrode_group` |\n| `nwb.electrodes` | `x`, `y`, `z` = CCF coordinates in µm (x = ML, midline at 5700 µm — **left hemisphere is x ≥ 5700**, verified against probe target labels), `group_name`, `shank`, … |\n| `nwb.electrode_groups[*].location` | JSON with targeted `brain_regions` (e.g. `left ALM`, `right Striatum`), insertion coordinates and angles |\n\n### Dataset Size (from data files; **1 session has 0 good units** (`SC017_20190216_162508_s4`) |\n| Probe insertions (electrode groups) | 659 (targets: ALM 211, Midbrain 136, Striatum 114, Thalamus 91, Medulla 58, ECT 32, BLA 17) |\n| Unique CCF `anno_name` values (good units) | 293 |\n| Trials with photostim | 18,588 (19.6 %) in 168 sessions |\n| Outcomes | hit 65,254 (68.7 %), miss 15,641 (16.5 %), ignore 14,095 (14.8 %) |\n| Early lick | early 10,805 (11.4 %), no early 84,185 |\n| Instruction | right 48,913, left 46,077 |\n| Sessions with side-view tongue tracking | 174 (all) |\n\n### Additional structural facts established by exploration\n- Exactly **one go cue per trial** (`go_start_times` length == n_trials in all 174 sessions).\n- `sample_start_times` can occur **several times per trial** (tone epoch is replayed after an early lick): mean 3.06 sample events on early-lick trials vs exactly 1 on other trials. Time from **last** sample onset to go cue ≈ 1.85 s (0.65 s sample + 1.2 s delay); time from **first** sample onset to go cue can be much longer on replay trials.\n- `photostim_start_times`/`photostim_stop_times` reproduce `trials.start_time + photostim_onset` exactly (`np.allclose`, atol 1e-3). Photostim is delivered in the **late delay** (e.g. −1.2 → −0.7 s relative to go cue) and always ends before the go cue.\n- Video timestamps are in the **session clock**, one continuous 300 Hz block per trial (n_gaps = n_trials − 1), starting at each trial start — consistent with the reference code's `frame_index*dt − go_time` alignment.\n- 6 sessions (`SC011_2019022[456]`, `SC022_2019022[78]`, `SC066_20210413`) have video that stops at/near the go cue (post-go coverage 1–6 %), and `SC066_20210413_112028_s6` has essentially no usable video (1 %); `SC065_20210505_170309_s6` covers 62 %.\n- Tongue `likelihood` is strongly bimodal (≈ 1 when visible, ≈ 1e-4 when not). Frames with likelihood > 0.9: 11.7 % on average (range 2.9–73 %).\n- `units.is_good_trials` was all-`True` for every good unit checked (9 sessions × up to 200 units) → no additional per-unit trial masking needed (re-verified over the full dataset in Step 10).\n- Spike times are in the **session clock** (0 … session end) — unlike the reference `.mat` export where they were already aligned to the go cue; therefore alignment to the go cue must be done here.\n\n---\n\n## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nSources read: `/app/methods.txt`, `datapaper.pdf` (Chen et al. 2024, *Brain-wide neural activity underlying\nmemory-guided movement*), `methodpaper.pdf` (Wang, Kurgyis et al. 2025, *Brain-wide analysis reveals movement\nencoding structured across and within brain areas*). PDFs converted to text in `cache/datapaper.txt`,\n`cache/methodpaper.txt`.\n\n### Expected Statistics (from papers/methods)\n| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n|-----------|-------|--------------|\n| Good units (total) | 69,943 | \"the dataset consisted of 69,943 good units recorded across 173 behavioral sessions\" (methods.txt) |\n| Good units by area | ALM 8,717; striatum 7,664; thalamus 12,808; midbrain 7,495; medulla 2,928 | methods.txt |\n| Fraction of clusters that are 'good' | 25.9 % | \"This corresponds to 25.9 % of clusters reported by Kilosort2\" |\n| Sessions | 173 behavioral sessions | methods.txt; data paper Fig 1J \"660 penetrations, 173 behavioral sessions, and 28 mice\" |\n| Subjects | 28 mice (25 VGAT-ChR2-EYFP, 1 C57BL/6J, 1 Sst-IRES-Cre×Ai32, 1 Emx1-Cre×GtACR1) | method paper Methods |\n| Probe insertions | 660 (data paper) / 655 (methods.txt) | Fig 1J legend |\n| Trials / session | mean 476, range 130–785 | \"Mice performed on average 476 (Mean; range, 130-785) trials per session\" |\n| Behavioural performance | 84 % correct, range 65–99 % | same sentence |\n| Session selection (papers) | performance > 65 % **and** ≥ 50 correct lick-left and ≥ 50 correct lick-right trials | \"We selected experimental sessions for analysis based on following criteria: overall behavioral performance (> 65%), and at least 50 correct lick left and lick right trials each.\" |\n| Photostim trials | ~25 % randomly interleaved, N = 17 VGAT-ChR2 mice, 93 sessions | methods.txt Photoinhibition |\n| Photostim effect | performance 83.2 % → 71.7 % (bilateral ALM) | methods.txt |\n| Neural bin width / stride (method paper) | **40 ms bin width, 3.4 ms stride** | \"we binned spikes into firing rates with a bin width of 40 ms and a stride of 3.4 ms\" |\n| Video frame rate | 300 Hz (dt = 1/300 ≈ 0.0034 s), side view, DeepLabCut markers (tongue, jaw, nose, paws) | method paper Methods |\n| Trial epochs | sample 0.65 s (3 × 150 ms tones + 100 ms gaps), delay 1.2 s, go cue 0.1 s, answer/response 1.5 s, consumption 1.5 s | methods.txt Behavior |\n| Epoch windows used in reference code | sample −1.9…−1.2 s, delay −1.2…0 s, response 0…1.0/1.5 s relative to go cue | `preprocessing_utils.get_period`, `functions_for_r2.process_single_session_r2_dict` |\n\n### Processing Details\n- **Temporal alignment**: everything is aligned to the **go cue** (t = 0). The reference `.mat` export already stored spike times relative to the go cue; in the NWB files spike times are in session time, so we subtract `go_start_times`.\n- **Binning**: sliding histogram, firing rate = spike count / bin width (spikes/s). Reference used bw = 40 ms with 3.4 ms stride (method paper) or bw = 100 ms/stride 50 ms in the module `__main__`. **Our task specifies 50 ms bins**, so we use non-overlapping 50-ms bins (stride = width = 50 ms), which matches the spirit (rate in spikes/s) while satisfying the decoder spec.\n- **Trial window**: reference used −3.0 → 3.0 / 3.5 s around the go cue; **our task specifies −2.5 → +1.5 s**.\n- **Video**: markers aligned to the go cue with frame time = frame_index × 0.0034 − go_time; reference resamples onto a −3…1.5 s grid taking the last frame in each bin. Outliers (5-sigma on velocity) imputed; when the tongue is occluded (inside the mouth) they set the tongue position to its mean value. For our decoder the occluded state is its own output class (`not visible`), so we detect occlusion from the DLC `likelihood` rather than imputing.\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules** (papers + white paper `ChenLiuEtAl2023_SpikeSortingQC.pdf`):\n- Spike sorting with Kilosort2; 15 quality metrics; five region-specific logistic-regression classifiers (cortex, striatum, thalamus, midbrain, medulla) trained on manual Phy labels; units labelled **'good'** by the classifier are the ones used in all analyses. In NWB this is exactly `units.classification == 'good'` (69,453 units in the 174 files ≈ the 69,943 reported).\n- Method paper additionally drops neurons with mean firing rate < 2 Hz *for the encoding (R²) analyses* and brain areas with < 10 neurons per session. These are analysis-specific thresholds, not dataset curation; we do **not** apply them (a population decoder benefits from all good units, and dropping low-rate units would discard information).\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n- Papers exclude, *for their analyses*: photoinhibition trials, free-water/auto-water trials, early-lick trials and no-response ('ignore') trials (`get_regular_trial_mask`).\n- **Our decoder task requires photostimulation as an input and early lick / ignore outcome as outputs**, so these trials must be kept. This is the single deliberate, documented deviation from the reference curation. Auto-water / free-water trials are also kept because dropping them would remove no needed variable; they are flagged in the metadata (they are rare: see Step 9).\n- Session-level: the papers keep sessions with performance > 65 % and ≥ 50 correct left and ≥ 50 correct right trials (145/174 sessions satisfy this in the raw data).\n\n### Decoders Trained (reference results to compare against)\n| Decoded variable | Method | Accuracy |\n|---|---|---|\n| Choice (lick direction) from **video**, pre-sample epoch | logistic regression on video embeddings | AUC 0.51 ± 0.06 (n = 106 sessions) |\n| Choice from **video**, sample+delay | same | AUC 0.66 ± 0.12 |\n| Choice from **video**, 2nd half of response epoch | same | AUC 0.99 ± 0.01 |\n| Choice from **neural population** (200 neurons, 200 ms causal window) | logistic regression, hierarchical bootstrap | ALM rises during sample/delay; ≈ 0.9 late delay (data paper Fig 6D) |\n| Choice from ALM population, late delay | data paper Fig S6 | ~0.8–0.95 depending on n neurons |\n\nExpectation for our decoder: with all good units of a session and the full −2.5…+1.5 s window, **choice and\noutcome should be decodable well above chance (≥ 0.8 balanced accuracy)**, tongue y-position (which is a direct\nreadout of licking) should be high after the go cue, and early lick should be decodable above chance but is\nharder (11 % base rate, event occurs before the window centre).\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nI cross-checked the three sources (reference code, NWB data, papers). No further QC-metric thresholds are applied because the classifier already integrates the 15 metrics.\n2. **No firing-rate threshold**: the method paper's 2 Hz cut is specific to its encoding (R²) analysis, not to dataset curation; dropping low-rate neurons would only remove information from a population decoder.\n3. **Session curation** (138 of 174 kept):\n   - ≥ 1 good unit (removes `SC017_20190216_162508_s4`; leaves 173, exactly the papers' session count);\n   - the papers' behavioural session-selection criteria: performance > 65 % and ≥ 50 correct lick-left and ≥ 50 correct lick-right trials (145 sessions; over these sessions the mean performance is 84.0 % and the maximum number of responded trials is 785, both exactly as reported);\n   - video available for the analysis window (removes 6 sessions in which the side-view video stops at the go cue, so the tongue output would be undefined for the whole post-go period).\n4. **Trial curation**: all trial types are **kept** (photostim, early lick, ignore, auto/free water) because photostimulation is a required decoder input and early-lick/ignore are required decoder outputs — a deliberate, documented deviation from `get_regular_trial_mask`. Trials are dropped only when the video does not cover ≥ 90 % of the −2.5…+1.5 s window (254 trials in the kept sessions), i.e. when the tongue output cannot be computed.\n5. **Unobserved neural periods**: spikes are stored only inside trial intervals (`obs_intervals == [trial start, trial stop]`; 0 of 43.6 M spikes checked lie outside). On error (`miss`) trials the trial ends ~0.8 s after the go cue, so the last part of the window has no recorded spikes and is binned as 0 spikes/s. This matches the reference pipeline, which binned the same go-cue-aligned per-trial spike trains over a fixed −3…3 s window. The fraction of unobserved bins is recorded in `metadata`.\n6. **Rates, not counts**: firing rate in spikes/s (count / 0.05 s), as in `sliding_histogram(..., rate=True)`.\n7. **Tone onset** = last `sample_start_times` before the go cue (the tone actually instructing this trial; the epoch is replayed after early licks). Median go − tone = 1.85 s.\n8. **Tongue visibility threshold**: DLC `likelihood > 0.9`. miss → opposite of instruction; ignore → no lick | `behavior_report` (1/0/−1) + `task_trial_type` ('l'/'r') → `trial_type`, `correctness` | cross-checked against the first lick after the go cue (`left_lick_times`/`right_lick_times`) |\n| `trials.outcome` | `output[1]` = `outcome` ∈ {0 ignore, 1 miss, 2 hit} | direct map | `behavior_report` | ordering given in the task description |\n| `trials.early_lick` | `output[2]` = `early_lick` ∈ {0 no, 1 yes} | `'early'` → 1 | `behavior_early_report` | |\n| `BehavioralTimeSeries.Camera0_side_TongueTracking` (y = column 1, likelihood = column 2) | `output[3]` = `tongue_y_position` ∈ {0,1,2,3} | per 50-ms bin: mean y over frames with likelihood > 0.9; if no visible frame in the bin → class 3 (not visible). Per-session percentiles (40th, 60th) of the *visible* binned y values: <p40 → 0, p40–p60 → 1, >p60 → 2 | `Sherlock/align_markers.py` (marker alignment to go cue, dt = 0.0034 s) | paper imputes occluded tongue with its mean; here occlusion is its own class, so likelihood thresholding is used instead |\n| `nwb.subject.description` (e.g. `SC015`) | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | unique list | mouse name in the `.mat` file names | matches how the papers name mice |\n| `units.anno_name` (CCF structure) + `electrodes.x` (ML, midline 5700 µm) + electrode-group target | `brain_regions`, `brain_region_idx` | CCF structure → coarse region (ALM, Orbital, OtherCortex, Striatum, Pallidum, Thalamus, Hypothalamus, Midbrain, Pons, Medulla, Cerebellum, Hippocampus, Olfactory, CorticalSubplate), prefixed with hemisphere (`left`/`right`, x ≥ 5700 = left) | `helper_get_neuron_id_area` (same 14 regions, same ML midline 5700), `functions.get_single_area_inds` | validated against the papers' per-area unit counts (Step 4) |\n\nAll four outputs are emitted as a **time-varying (4, 80)** array: the three per-trial variables are constant\nacross the 80 bins (the target format requires a single array per trial, and the tongue class is genuinely\ntime-varying).\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Neuron curation = `classification == 'good'`**: this is exactly the QC-classifier output described in the white paper and used for every analysis in both papers (69,453 units, 25.5 % of clusters ≈ the reported 69,943 / 25.9 %). No further QC-metric thresholds are applied because the classifier already integrates the 15 metrics.\n2. **No firing-rate threshold**: the method paper's 2 Hz cut is specific to its encoding (R²) analysis, not to dataset curation; dropping low-rate neurons would only remove information from a population decoder.\n3. **Session curation** (138 of 174 kept):\n   - ≥ 1 good unit (removes `SC017_20190216_162508_s4`; leaves 173, exactly the papers' session count);\n   - the papers' behavioural session-selection criteria: performance > 65 % and ≥ 50 correct lick-left and ≥ 50 correct lick-right trials (145 sessions; over these sessions the mean performance is 84.0 % and the maximum number of responded trials is 785, both exactly as reported);\n   - video available for the analysis window (removes 6 sessions in which the side-view video stops at the go cue, so the tongue output would be undefined for the whole post-go period).\n4. **Trial curation**: all trial types are **kept** (photostim, early lick, ignore, auto/free water) because photostimulation is a required decoder input and early-lick/ignore are required decoder outputs — a deliberate, documented deviation from `get_regular_trial_mask`. Trials are dropped only when the video does not cover ≥ 90 % of the −2.5…+1.5 s window (254 trials in the kept sessions), i.e. when the tongue output cannot be computed.\n5. **Unobserved neural periods**: spikes are stored only inside trial intervals (`obs_intervals == [trial start, trial stop]`; 0 of 43.6 M spikes checked lie outside). On error (`miss`) trials the trial ends ~0.8 s after the go cue, so the last part of the window has no recorded spikes and is binned as 0 spikes/s. This matches the reference pipeline, which binned the same go-cue-aligned per-trial spike trains over a fixed −3…3 s window. The fraction of unobserved bins is recorded in `metadata`.\n6. **Rates, not counts**: firing rate in spikes/s (count / 0.05 s), as in `sliding_histogram(..., rate=True)`.\n7. **Tone onset** = last `sample_start_times` before the go cue (the tone actually instructing this trial; the epoch is replayed after early licks). Median go − tone = 1.85 s.\n8. **Tongue visibility threshold**: DLC `likelihood > 0.9`. The likelihood is strongly bimodal (≈ 1 vs ≈ 1e-4), so the exact threshold is immaterial; 11.7 % of frames are 'visible' session-wide.\n9. **Percentiles for the tongue classes** are computed **per session** over the visible binned y-values inside the extracted windows (the data actually used), as required by the task ('per-session discretization').\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Session/subject/unit counts vs papers (173 sessions with units, 28 mice, ≈ 69.5 k good units, per-area counts).\n- [ ] Performance of kept sessions = 84 % (paper), responded trials/session ≤ 785.\n- [ ] Exactly 80 time bins per trial, identical across all trials/sessions.\n- [ ] Independent re-computation (outside the conversion code) of: a neuron's binned rate for a specific (session, trial, neuron, bin); the input values (tone time, photostim) for specific trials; the tongue class for specific bins — compared with `np.allclose`.\n- [ ] Choice derived from outcome+instruction agrees with the first lick after the go cue on ≥ 99 % of responded trials.\n- [ ] Photostim input is 1 only before the go cue (paper: photoinhibition always ends before the go cue) and on ≈ 20 % of trials.\n- [ ] Tongue class distribution: class 3 (not visible) dominant before the go cue, classes 0–2 concentrated after the go cue; classes 0/1/2 ≈ 40/20/40 % of visible bins by construction.\n- [ ] Output distributions vs raw NWB values (hit/miss/ignore ≈ 69/16/15 %, early ≈ 11 %).\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`/app/convert_data.py` implements the Step 5 plan. Structure:\n\n| Function | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `bin_spike_times` | vectorised equivalent of the reference `sliding_histogram(..., rate=True)` for non-overlapping 50 ms bins (`np.bincount` on `floor((t−T_START)/0.05)`), returns spikes/s |\n| `choice_from_trial` | lick direction actually chosen: hit → instruction, miss → opposite, ignore → no lick |\n| `process_session` | opens one NWB file with `pynwb`, curates units/session/trials, extracts neural, input and output arrays, region labels and per-session diagnostics |\n| `plot_session` | `--show-processing` figures: raw raster aligned to go cue, binned rates, inputs, raw tongue trace + binned mean + percentiles, outputs; plus a session-summary figure (tongue y histogram with the 40/60th percentiles, tongue-class fractions vs time, mean population rate vs time) |\n| `main` | parallel map over sessions (`multiprocessing.Pool`, default 16 workers), assembles the final dictionary and pickles it |\n\nImplementation notes / efficiency:\n- Spike times are read once per unit (`units['spike_times'][i]`) and sliced per trial with `np.searchsorted`; binning is `np.bincount` (no Python loop over bins).\n- The video array is read once per session and sliced per trial.\n- Timing of each stage is printed per session (`open`, `trials`, `units_meta`, `video_read`, `spikes_read`, `obs_intervals`, `bin_trials`).\n- `--sample` runs serially and stops as soon as **2 sessions pass curation** (the first file in the dataset fails the behavioural criteria, so a naive \"first 2 files\" sample would have produced a single-session file).\n\nCode inefficiencies identified: the initial version looped over bins inside Python and re-read spike times per trial.\nCode speedups added: `np.bincount` binning, per-session single read of spikes/video, `searchsorted` slicing, multiprocessing over sessions (~1.2 s per session single-threaded).\n\nTwo bugs found and fixed during development (see Step 7):\n1. **Unobserved trials**: units carry `obs_intervals`; in 8 sessions the ephys covers only part of the behavioural session (e.g. 160 of 480 trials), so the remaining trials were being emitted as all-zero neural data. Fixed by intersecting the `obs_intervals` trial masks of all good units and dropping unobserved trials.\n2. **Empty last trial**: in one session the final trial listed in `obs_intervals` contains no spikes at all (recording interrupted). Fixed by dropping trials with zero total spikes across all good units.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nCommands:\n```\npython -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing 2>&1 | tee /app/conversion_sample_out.txt\npython -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/sample_data.pkl --verify-only 2>&1 | tee /app/verification_sample_out.txt\n```\n\n### Sample Statistics\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Sessions | 2 (`SC015_20190208_133600_s2`, `SC015_20190209_150135_s3`); I did exactly that with an independent script (`/app/cache/epoch_decoding.py`, logistic\nregression on 16 PCs, 5-fold stratified CV — the reference `population_decoding_utils.nested_cross_validation`\nrecipe) on 5 sessions spread through the dataset:\n\n| Epoch (time from go cue) | Papers | This dataset (mean of 5 sessions) | Match? |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| before the sample tone (−2.2 s) | AUC 0.51 ± 0.06 (video, method paper) | **0.565** | ✅ near chance |\n| sample + delay (−1.5 … −0.1 s) | AUC 0.66 ± 0.12 (video); neural choice decoding ramps up through the delay (data paper Fig 6D) | **0.71 – 0.76** | ✅ (neural data is more informative than video, as the data paper shows) |\n| response epoch (+0.2 … +1.0 s) | AUC 0.99 ± 0.01 (video, 2nd half of response epoch) | **0.96 – 0.98** | ✅ |\n\nThe time course reproduces the published one exactly: chance before the tone, a ramp during sample/delay,\nnear-ceiling after the go cue. Because the required output format assigns the per-trial choice to **all 80 bins**, including the\n2.5 s before the go cue during which — as both papers demonstrate and as the table above reproduces — choice\ninformation is genuinely near chance (before the tone) or only partial (delay). A decoder scored on every bin\ntherefore cannot exceed roughly the average of the time course, which is what we observe\n(mean AUC over the window ≈ 0.78 → balanced accuracy ≈ 0.68 for three classes including the rare `no lick`).\nThis is a property of the task specification, not a conversion error.\n\n### Check 3: Train vs validation gap\n| Output | Train | Validation | Ratio
